<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, March 9, 2021
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, March 9, 2021, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant
to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
We continue to maintain physical distancing and will conduct Board of Supervisor meetings virtually by zoom. Board
Members, staff and members of the public may call and participate in the meeting by following these steps:
To Join Zoom Meeting now, click here:  https://zoom.us/j/92740255842?pwd=QmxiT0t5b04zWTc1QUgza2toVzlaQT09 
 
Web Conference:
Call In Number: 1-253-215-8782 or 1-346-248-7799 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 927 4025 5842
Password: 073495
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions: 
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov 1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by March 8, 2021, by 5:00 pm. for the March 9, 2021, Board meeting.4.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up
to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: February 23, 2021  
 
b. Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, November & December 2020  
 
c. Sympathy letter to Clifton Foster (IT), Don Wells (IT) and Judge Ralph Hatch (Superior
Court)
 
 
d. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 February 2021

<!-- page 2 -->
e. Minor Revisions/Additions to the Navajo County Personnel Policy Manual  
 
f. Revision to the Navajo County Bereavement Policy  
 
g. Revisions and updates to the Navajo County Travel Policy  
 
h. Amendment No. 1 extending Contract No. B20-01-007 with The Mahoney Group for
one additional year (April , 2021, through March 31, 2022)
 
 
i. Application for the Gila River Indian Community grant opportunity in an effort to add up to
3 additional fleet vehicles for the Navajo County Sheriff's Office
 
 
j. Acceptance of the Governor's Office of Highway Safety Contract 2021-207-054 in the
amount of $19,177 through the Proposition 207 funding
 
 
k. Appointment of David Widmaier as a Justice of the Peace Pro Tempore pursuant to A.R.S.
§22-121 and as a Juvenile Hearing Officer pursuant to A.R.S. §8-323A&B through
December 31, 2021
 
 
l. Gila River Indian Community Grant Application for Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District
for an Ambulance and Gurney Addition
 
 
m. Appointment of Martha Pearce and Jonathan Michael Nez as Democratic Precinct
Committeemen for their respective precincts
 
 
n. Appointment of Taber Heisler for a two-year term and re-appointment of Randy Murph for a
four-year term to the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission
 
 
3. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. PLANNING & ZONING:  
 
1. Presentation to Chuck Teetsel for his Years of Service as Chairman on the Planning and
Zoning Commission
 
Presented By: John Osgood/Supervisor Whitesinger
 
b. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Contract

<!-- page 3 -->
a. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Contract
with Woodson Engineering & Surveying for Summer Pines Drainage Analysis and Contract
with Entellus for Timberland Acres Hydrology and Hydraulics 
 
 
4. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. COVID-19 Update  
Presented By: Janelle Linn
 
5. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
6. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: March 5, 2021 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 10:15 am By:MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Minor Revisions/Additions to the Navajo County Personnel Policy Manual
Background:

have operated with a 30-day waiting period for some time, need to update policy to better reflect

2. Sheriff's Office Step Progression - Addition to step policy to have Sworn Sheriff positions as an

November 2020 
 
2.5     HEALTH INSURANCE AND BENEFITS 
 
2.51    Policy: 
 
Medical, Dental, Vision, Life Insurance and other benefits as approved by the Board of 
Supervisors are provided to all eligible employees through the County’s Health Trust  with 
dependent coverage being an option.  Coverage requires contribution through payroll deductions.  
Information will be provided to new employees during orientation and on an as needed basis 
throughout the year. 
 
2.52 Effective Date 
 
New full-time employees scheduled to work 30 hours or more each week will be covered on the 
first day of  the month following a thirty day waiting period. The thirty day waiting period 
begins the first day of hire or first day of benefit eligible status. Employee contributions are for 
current month coverages and will be deducted from the first and second payrolls of each month 
for a total of 24 contributions.

<!-- page 421 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
D. Schedule for Progression within Salary Range 
 
Employees shall move to the next Step within the Range currently held every two 
(2) years on their anniversary date.  Employees who reach the maximum of their 
Range (Step 10) will receive a one-time increase equivalent to the difference 
between Step 9 and Step 10 of their current Range every two (2) years on their 
anniversary date. 
 
Employees must meet the minimum performance requirements of their position to 
be eligible to move to the next S tep within the Range.  Employees under a 
Performance Improvement Plan or disciplinary action must return to normal work 
status and meet the minimum performance requirements of their position before 
being moved to the next Step within the Range.  Movement t o the next Step will 
be made effective from when the employee meets minimum performance 
requirements of the position, and will not be made retroactive from the employee’s 
anniversary date.  
 
In cases where an employee has recently received a salary increas e or will soon 
receive a salary increase not related to their scheduled Step Progression, the 
Elected Official or Department Director will coordinate with Human Resources to 
determine the appropriateness and timeliness of the next scheduled Step 
Increase.   
 
The exception to this policy is the Sheriff’s Office sworn positions, who 
follow a separate Salary step schedule. 
 
D. Lateral Transfers:   
 
An employee transferred to a position in the same Range or to any other 
classification in the same Range, shall b e paid the same salary (same  Step in 
range currently held) that was received prior to the transfer.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 03/09/2021  
Title: Personnel Policy revision/additions
Submitted For: Eric Scott, HR Director  Submitted By: Eric Scott, HR
Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Human Resources
Motion before the Board:
Revision to the Navajo County Bereavement Policy
Background:
Navajo County's current bereavement policy allows for use of an employee's sick leave to cover
bereavement leave. The proposed revision of this policy would allow for paid bereavement leave
up to 3 days, or 5 days for out of state travel, per calendar year. The revision would not require
employees to use their own sick or annual leave, but would be a benefit to the employee. This
revision is consistent with many other entities' practice.

<!-- page 423 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Proposal to change bereavement policy March 2021: 
Current policy: 
 
Proposed: 
3.28 Bereavement 
Navajo County offers bereavement leave to provide a time for mourning after the loss of an 
immediate family member of an employee, or that of their spouse or domestic partner. For 
purposes of this policy, immediate family is defined as: Spouse, domestic partner, mother, 
father, child, stepchild, foster child, domestic partner’s child, grandchild, grandparent, brother, 
sister, mother in-law, father in law, sister in-law, brother in-law, grandparent in-law, and 
stepparent of the employee. Elected Officials and Department Directors may give special 
consideration to any other person whose association with the employee was similar to any of 
the above relationships.  
An employee may be absent with pay for a period of time not to exceed three (3) working days 
or five (5) days for out-of-state travel per calendar year for an occurrence of death of an 
immediate family member.  If additional time is needed, vacation or personal time may be taken 
with supervisory approval.  
Exceptions to this policy may be approved by the County Manager.

<!-- page 426 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
6.1 Introduction 
 
The Navajo County Travel Policy is to be used for all authorized County travel. Non - 
compliance with the Navajo County Travel Policy including the improper claim of 
expenses may result in appropriate disciplinary action up to and including termination. 
 
The purpose of the Navajo County Travel Policy is to ensure: 
 
A. Compliance with State of Arizona Revised Statutes. 
 
B. Compliance with applicable sections of the U.S. Internal Revenue Code. 
 
C. Travel expenses incurred while conducting official business of Navajo County are 
appropriate and necessary. 
 
D. Reasonable accommodations and expense reimbursements are provided to 
Navajo County employees. 
 
E. Compliance with the County’s formally adopted budget. 
 
6.2 Statutory Authority 
 
Arizona statutes allow for the reimbursement of travel expenses for state employees and 
officers under A.R.S. §38 -621 thru §38 -637. Navajo County generally follows the 
provisions of these statutes. 
 
6.3 Definitions 
 
A. Authorized travel – Travel for official County business that has been budgeted and 
approved by the appropriate Elected Official, Department Director or the County 
Manager. 
 
B. Conference Designated Lodging  - The hotel(s) where a confer ence, convention, 
seminar, training, affiliate or association meeting is being held as specified in the 
brochure. Accommodations at alternate hotels in the immediate vicinity of these 
functions may be considered as conference designated lodging when no 
vacancies exist at the recommended hotel(s). 
 
C. Duty Post - The place an officer or employee spends the largest portion of his 
regular working time or the place to which they return on completion of a special 
assignment. An employee who has more than one place of work on a regular basis 
is deemed to have multiple duty posts. In accordance with A.R.S §11-215 the duty 
post for members of the Board of Supervisors shall be their principal residence. 
The duty post for members of other boards, commissions, advis ory committees 
and volunteers who are not employees of the County shall be their principal 
residence.

<!-- page 427 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
(6.3 Cont.) 
 
D. Normal Work Day - The typical period of time an employee is at work. 
 
E. Business Meal - A meal that is consumed while conducting official County 
business.   
 
F. Per Diem - The maximum daily allowance for meals and travel related expenses. 
 
G. Travel Status - An employee is considered to be on travel status when traveling on 
County business outside of Navajo County and/or more than fifty miles from their 
duty post or personal residence if closer to the destination. 
 
H. Local Mileage - Personal vehicle mileage that is eligible for reimbursement when 
an employee is not on travel status. 
 
I. Conference – Refers to conferences, conventions, seminars, trainings, and affiliate 
or association meetings. 
 
J. Volunteer - A person that is giving their time, assistance and expertise to the county 
without compensation. 
 
6.4 Travel Policy 
 
A. All authorized travelers may claim expenses as provided in this policy. 
 
B. Maximum Mileage, Lodging and Meal, Parking and Incidental Expense  
Reimbursement rates are established by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
(see Section 6.10). 
 
C. The Elected Official, Department Director or their designee must authorize all 
employee travel prior to the travel date . A pre -trip form must be filled out and 
approved by the approving authority before travel arrangements are made  
(Elected Officials are n ot required to fill out the pre -trip form unless they are 
requesting a per diem advance payment) . All out-of-State travel must have prior 
approval of the Elected Official, Assistant County Manager or County Manager. 
 
D. A Navajo County purchasing card is the preferred method of payment for all 
authorized travel expenditures except meals (see Section 6.8.D.1 for information 
on per diem meal reimbursements) .  Purchasing cards should only be used to 
pay for the travel expenditures of the employee or elected official.  
 
E. Each department is responsible for providing direction and information concerning 
travel to their personnel, subject to the limitations of the travel policy. 
 
F. The Elected Official or County Manager may authorize exceptions to the provisions 
of this travel policy for extraordinary circumstances. Such exceptions must be 
documented.

<!-- page 428 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
6.5 Conferences 
A. Registration expenses for conferences should be paid with a purchasing card in 
advance. When purchasing cards are not accepted a purchase order for the 
registration should be submitted early enough for routine processing through the 
accounts payable system. 
 
B. Lodging at a confe rence hotel may be for an amount in excess of the Maximum 
Travel Reimbursement Rate if approved by the  Department Director, Assistant 
County Manager, County Manager or Elected Official. 
 
6.6 Transportation 
 
A. Allowable expenses include common carrier fares, priv ately owned motor vehicle 
mileage, bridge and road tolls, necessary taxi, bus or streetcar fares, motor vehicle 
rental, and reasonable baggage handling expenses. 
 
B. The means of transportation  should be the most economical and in the best 
interests of the County. Any portion of travel by an indirect route or for personal 
business is not an allowable expense. Excess travel time must be charged to 
annual leave. 
 
C. A valid Arizona driver's license  is required if an employee is driving any motor 
vehicle while on County business. The employee must enter their driver’s license 
number on the Employee Travel Form. 
 
D. County Vehicles 
 
1. County vehicle use is preferred when practical. Elected Officials and 
Department Directors may approve the use of private vehicles when 
necessary. Local mileage may be approved for employees not on travel 
status to conduct county business if a county vehicle is not available. 
2. Out-of-state use of County-owned motor vehicles must be approved by the 
Elected Official or County Manager. 
3. County fuel and maintenance facilities  should be used whenever possible. 
County purchasing cards may be used for the purchase of fuel, oil and minor 
repairs of County vehicles if necessary. Each department is responsible for 
the costs of operating their County -owned motor vehicles, including fuel, 
parking, maintenance and repairs. 
 
E. Privately-Owned Motor Vehicle 
 
1. Out-of-state travel using a privately owned motor vehicle may be allowed 
with approval by the Elected Official or County Manager. Mileage will be 
authorized at the lesser of either the mileage times the adopted 
mileage

<!-- page 429 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
(6.6 Cont.) 
rate or air coach fare to the same destination plus applicable ground 
transportation costs. If a privately -owned vehicle for out -of-state travel is 
used for the convenience of the employee per diem should not exceed 
amounts allowed by the most efficient means of travel. Any additional time 
taken to drive a private vehicle out-of-state must be taken as annual leave. 
 
 
2.  Mileage reimbursements may be authorized for in-state travel at the current 
mileage reimbursement rate (Section 6.10). Mileage can be calculated 
using actual odometer readings, official maps or internet mileage 
calculations. Documentation supporting mileage should be included with 
the Employee Travel Form. 
3. Purchasing cards are not to be used to purchase fuel for privately owned 
vehicles. 
4. Personal insurance  is required f or all employees operating a privately - 
owned vehicle on County business as required by ARS §28 - 4135. If an 
employee does not carry motor vehicle liability they will be prohibited from 
using their privately-owned motor vehicle on County business. 
5. Liability for an accident in a privately owned motor vehicle while on County 
business is covered by the employee’s liability insurance policy. If the 
accident liability exceeds their personal liability coverage the County's 
insurance program may provide some cover age. The County will not 
reimburse an employee for any physical damage to a personal motor 
vehicle involved in an accident, regardless of fault. 
 
F. Rented Motor Vehicles 
 
1. Rented motor vehicles must be for the benefit of the County and not for the 
personal co nvenience of the employee. The Elected Official or  
Department Director is responsible for determining that a rented motor 
vehicle is the best option. 
2. When an employee rents a motor vehicle on County business they are 
automatically insured for liability and physical damage losses. Therefore, 
rented motor vehicle insurance costs incurred by the traveler are not 
reimbursable. 
3. Gasoline purchases should be made with a purchasing card. 
 
G. Airlines 
 
1. Allowable expenses for commercial air travel shall be limited to the lowest 
fare available. The cost may exceed the lowest fare if air travel at a higher 
cost results in a clear benefit to the County. 
2. Flight insurance can only be purchased at the employee’s expense.

<!-- page 430 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
(6.6 Cont.) 
3. Commuter flights from local areas to Phoenix are an acceptable mode of 
travel when such travel is prudent and reasonable. Commuter flights must 
be pre-approved by the Elected Official or Department Director. 
 
4. When travel is cancelled, any airfare credit issued should be u sed on the 
very next trip before the expiration date. 
 
H. Chartered Aircraft 
 
1. Chartered aircraft travel is allowed provided a common carrier is not feasible 
or an alternate mode of travel is impractical. 
 
2. Use of chartered aircraft must be pre-approved by the Elected Official or 
County Manager. 
 
3. Chartered air service may only be obtained from FAA licensed commercial 
flight operators. 
 
I. Privately Owned Aircraft  - Requires prior Elected Official or County Manager 
approval. Mileage reimbursement s are based on statute miles at adopted rates 
(see Section 6.10). Mileage should be determined using the most direct air route. 
Landing and parking fees are allowed except at the location where the aircraft is 
normally based. 
 
J.  Railroads and Buses  - Railroad or bus travel may be used when convenient or 
economical and their use will not involve excessive travel time. 
 
K. Local Transportation, Tolls and Parking - Taxi, bus, Uber/Lift, limousine and 
streetcar use is classified as local transportation and can be reimbursed. Other 
expenses such as tolls and parking are also reimbursable  (see Section 6.10 for 
maximum parking rates). Purchasing cards should be used where possible. 
 
L. County Liability Insurance 
 
1. County Administration has established programs to handle all claims for 
property damage, public liability and workers' compensation involving the 
County, its property and employees. 
2. Should an employee become involved in an accident that results in damage 
to County property, damage to property of others, bodily injury or some 
other form of damage to an individual or organization, the situation should 
be reported immediately by phone to administration. A full written report 
must be submitted to admini stration on the risk management incident 
form. Personal injury must be reported on the Workers Compensation  
form.

<!-- page 431 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
(6.6 Cont.) 
 
3. In addition to the above information, the employee directly or indirectly 
involved in an accident, shall not talk about the acc ident to anyone other 
than the appropriate individual(s) within their department, administration 
or an attorney representing the County. Other than these people, no 
discussion should take place concerning the accident, nor should any 
reports be given to any other individuals. 
 
6.7 Lodging 
 
A. In-County lodging will not be reimbursed unless (1) an emergency exists and 
lodging is approved by the Elected Official or Department Director or (2) lodging 
costs are associated with a conference being held in County and is more than 50 
miles away from the employee’s home. 
 
B. Out-of-County lodging may be authorized when the employee is in travel status 
and their work day is extended beyond 13 hours. 
 
C. Lodging must be at a commercial establishment at the lowest available rate such 
as state government, corporate, commercial or internet special. Maximum 
allowable lodging rates are included in Section 6.10. 
 
D. Reimbursement for lodging shared with an individual not on County business 
(such as a spouse) will be at the single room rate. 
 
6.8 Meals 
 
A. Eligibility for authorized meal expense reimbursement  - Employees must be in 
authorized travel status to be eligible for meals. Authorized meal expense is 
limited to the daily per diem amount.  Employees must be in travel status a 
minimum of five hours to be eligible for meal allowances. 
 
B. Travel Advances – Travel advances will be issued for per diem amounts only upon 
prior approval of the Department Director, Elected Official, Assistant County 
Manager or County Manager. 
 
C. Meals provided by Hotel, Meeting or Conference - No allowance will be authorized 
when meals are provided by the hotel or organization as a part of a conference  or 
meeting. 
 
D. Meals provided while traveling without an overnight stay is taxable for 
reimbursements. Meals provided while on travel status with an overnight stay is 
not taxable. 
 
1. Receipts are not required for per diem meal reimbursements.  See 6.10.C 
for maximum meal rates.  If a county credit card is used for meals, 
itemized receipts are required and payment i s the amount actually spent 
for a meal, not to exceed the maximum per diem rate.   If an employee

<!-- page 432 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
chooses to submit receipts with the Employee Travel Form, the receipts 
must be itemized, the meals are subject to the breakfast, lunch & dinner 
rates and only for actual costs incurred.   
 
(6.8 Cont.) 
 
2. First & Last Day Travel – Meals will be limited to 75% of the daily per diem 
rate on the first and last day of the employee’s travel status. 
3. Overnight Travel within Navajo County – If travel is authorized under Travel 
Policy Section 6.7(A) meals will be allowed. 
4. Emergency Meals  - Under emergency circumstances meals or meal 
allowances may be provided by Navajo County with the approval of the 
Elected Official or County Manager. 
5. Business Meals - When required to conduct official County business prior 
approval of the Elected Official or County Manager must be obtained. 
Documentation to justify the validity of a business meal should include a list 
the names of the persons who attended the meeting and an agenda or 
statement as to the business purpose of the meeting. 
6. Volunteer Reimbursements - In accordance with A.R.S §11 -251(49) the 
Planning & Zoning Commission, Board Of Adjustment, Workforce 
Investment Board and other boards comprised of vo lunteer members are 
authorized per diem at the dinner rate, regardless of the meeting time. 
Round trip mileage to a meeting from their personal residence is also 
allowed. See Section 6.10 for meal and mileage reimbursement rates. 
7. Tips – Reasonable tips may be allowed and are included in the maximum 
meal reimbursement rates (see 6.10(C)). 
 
6.9 TRAVEL RECONCILIATION 
 
A. The Elected Official or Department Director or their designee should ensure: 
 
1. That all travel expenditures are within their adopted budget. 
2. The travel complies with the County Travel Policy and has been properly 
approved. 
3. Adequate travel documentation is maintained. 
 
a. A commercial establishment's original purchase order, invoice and 
receipts as applicable are required for travel expenses including 
conference registrations, transportation, lodging, and other 
miscellaneous charges. 
b. Copies of all registration forms, meeting schedules, brochures or 
agendas shall be submitted with the Travel Form. 
c. Copies of purchase made with the County purchasing card can be 
included with the Travel Form. Original P-card receipts are retained 
with the monthly P-card reconciliation. 
 
4. The Employee Travel Form is properly completed and signed by the 
employee and department official. 
5. The original Employee Tr avel Form including supporting documentation is 
maintained by the department. An electronic copy of the Employee Travel

<!-- page 433 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Form and supporting documentation will be maintained on-line for audit and 
review purposes. 
 
 
(6.9 Cont.) 
 
6. When a travel reimbursement amount is due to an employee a copy of the 
Employee travel form will be submitted to Finance where the payment will 
be processed promptly through the County’s accounts payable  system.  
IRS guidelines require that requests for reimbursements be made in a 
timely manner.  It will be considered timely if employees receive advances 
within 30 days before the time that they incur the expense, and adequately 
account for the expense within 60 days after completion of travel.  Failure 
to comply with this requiremen t will jeopardize the County reimbursement 
plan status as an accountable plan.  Payments to employees for travel 
and other necessary expenses under a non -accountable plan are 
considered wages and must be treated as supplemental wages and are 
subject to inc ome tax withholding and payment of social security, 
Medicare and FUTA taxes.  Any request for reimbursement received after 
60 days after completion of travel wi ll be considered taxable income to the 
employee, reimbursable through payroll, and be  reported o n the 
employee’s Form W-2. 
 
7. Per IRS guidelines, excess reimbursement  due to the County are  to be  
repaid within 120 days after the expense was paid or incurred . If amounts 
are not repaid, they may be withheld from the employee’s bi -weekly 
payroll. 
 
8. Non-reimbursable expenses cannot be claimed.  The following expenses 
are examples of non -reimbursable expenses:  in -room movies, alcohol, 
and personal entertainment. 
 
 
6.10 Maximum Travel Reimbursement Rates – 
 
A. For meal rates/breakdowns, maximum hotel lodging rates, the personal 
vehicle mileage reimbursement rate, and privately -owned aircraft rate, 
please visit the Arizona Department of Administration website: 
 
https://gao.az.gov/travel/welcome-gao-travel 
 
Click on “Current Arizona State Reimbursement Rates”, Go to “50 Travel”; click 
on “Current Arizona State Reimbursement Rates” and click on “Section 95: 
Maximum mileage, lodging, meal, parking and incidental expense 
reimbursement rates”. Meal and hotel allowances vary by location ; if a city not 
listed is in a county whose rate is listed, then the county’s rate applies.

Narrative 
Please structure your proposal to provide the following information in the order indicated.  Provide the narrative in 
paragraph form in the text field provided.  Please be thorough but strive for brevity. 
1. Briefly describe your organization’s history, mission and goals. 
Navajo County is in the Northeastern portion of Arizona. As of the 2010 census, the population of Navajo 
County was 107,449. ln the summer months, the population often swells to over 3OO,OOO residents,  many 
who own seasonal homes within the county. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office serves the citizens and visitors 
of Navajo County, Arizona. Navajo County is a culturally rich environment where the citizens of the county 
reside from the high desert areas of Arizona to the mountainous regions of the White Mountains. The county 
includes parts of the Hopi Indian Reservation, the Navajo Nation, The White Mountain Apache Tribe 
and borders near the San Carlos Apache Tribe. Currently, the Navajo County Sheriff's Office has fifty -seven 
certified (sworn) law enforcement officers that respond to calls for service within the county. Comprised of 
two districts, the Sheriff's Office is responsible for providing law enforcement services to the unincorporated 
areas of Winslow, Holbrook, Joseph City, Snowflake, Taylor, Heber, Overgaard, Pinedale, Pinetop, Lakeside, 
Show Low, Clay Springs, Aripine, Linden, Summer Pines, Show Low Pines, Wagon Wheel, Adamana, White 
Mountain Lakes, Silver Lakes Estates, Concho, Antelope Valley, Chevelon Retreat, Sky-Hi Retreat, Sun Valley, 
Woodruff and Shumway. Additionally, the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office provides law enforcement assistance 
for the Coconino Sheriff’s Office, Navajo Nation Police Department, Hopi Police Department, Hopi Rangers, 
and the White Mountain Apache Police Department. Navajo County has a total area of 9,960 square miles 
within its boundaries. The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office has a very close working relationship with our tribal 
partners. The Sheriff’s Office routinely conducts firearm and other classroom training with and for the Hopi 
Tribe. Additionally, the Sheriff's Office provides detention officer basic training to surrounding tribes such as 
the San Carlos Apache and Hopi Tribes. We have a dedicated Sheriff Deputy, who works the Pinon and 
Kayenta areas in the Navajo Nation and whose primary responsibility is county and state assistance to our 
tribal partners. The Sheriff’s Office also contributes financial support to area Native American tribes in the 
form of High Intensity Drug Trafficking Area (HITDA) grant funding. Our office commonly sends K-9 drug dogs 
to assist area tribes in our common goal of ridding the area of drug trafficking. Our Office Major Crimes 
Apprehension Team also commonly sends a deputy to educate Navajo Nation schools on drug awareness. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
2. To determine eligibility for this grant, if the applying organization is non-municipal or a non-profit 501c, please 
describe your relationship with Arizona municipalities including past and present support, whether through

<!-- page 442 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2021 
 
3 
 
partnerships, collaborations, in-kind contributions, grants, tax-based funding or other.  Describe the supporting 
municipality’s role in this project.  If the applying organization is a municipality or a municipal sub-division please 
enter the text “Not applicable” and go to item 3. 
Not applicable  
3. Describe the proposed project and your plan to implement. 
The Navajo County Sheriff’s Office seeks to obtain critically needed grant funding to replace a portion of our 
existing high mileage reservation patrol vehicles. Eighteen of our existing patrol and K-9 vehicles need to be 
replaced immediately. A total of three Chevy Tahoe SUVs (one outfitted as a K-9 Vehicle and two outfitted as 
patrol vehicles) are being requested. This proposal fulfills the required "priority areas" identified by the Gila 
River Indian Community as our agency is a certified law enforcement agency in the State of Arizona. 
 
4. Describe how the proposed project satisfies one or more of the priority funding areas identified by the Gila River 
Indian Community. 
The goal of this new project is intended to enhance the public safety goal of Navajo County, which is to 
preserve and protect. New vehicles ensure reliable, safe and timely responses to emergency and routine calls  
for service. This goal will be met by maintaining and improving our response time to all generated calls.  
 
5. Identify the needs/problems to be addressed, target population and number of people to be served by the 
project. 
ln calendar year 2020, the Navajo County Sheriff Office responded to, or initiated, 28,687 recorded incidents 
throughout the county. Due to the rural nature of law enforcement within the county, deputies routinely drive 
several hundred miles each work week, often in adverse weather conditions and difficult terrain. For this 
reason the large majority of patrol vehicles in the Sheriff's Office have very high mileage, many upwards of 
200,000 miles. Due to current fiscal conditions within Navajo County, the Sheriff Office is struggling to replace 
vehicles in order to provide adequate law enforcement services countywide.  
 
The approval of this grant proposal by the Gila River Indian Community will help ensure the citizens of Navajo  
County and surrounding agencies which NCSO consistently assists, continue to receive quality law 
enforcement services. Possessing vehicles that are reliable and not plagued with frequent mechanical 
problems is a critical goal for the county management team at present time. 
 
Regarding the specific need for vehicles of this type, many of the county roads are remote and unpaved, and 
winter weather conditions make it necessary to have a large contingency of four-wheel drive vehicles in our

<!-- page 443 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2021 
 
4 
 
fleet. We are seeking the funding to purchase a maximum of two 2021 Chevy Tahoe’s that will be fully 
outfitted as patrol vehicles as well as 1 K9 (1 K9 vehicle, 2 Patrol vehicles). 
 
6. Define the project as a new or continuing program.  Has GRIC previously funded this project? 
New, Navajo County received funding in the past (FY2013 and FY2015) for vehicles, however this particular 
project would be considered new. 
7. Provide a brief timeline including start and finish dates.  Indicate if the timeline is flexible. 
If granted, the Navajo County Sheriff Office will immediately order these needed vehicles. It is our hope that 
the vehicles would be fully outfitted and available for use no later than November of 2021, however with the 
pandemic, it would appear that it is taking longer to receive product. The  average life span of one vehicle is 
between three and four calendar years with routine maintenance provided which allows these vehicles to be 
sustained for usage well past the factory warranty period.  
 
8. Identify other organizations, partners or funders participating in the project and their roles. 
Currently there are no other partners or funders participating in this project other than the Navajo County 
Sheriff’s Office. 
 
9. Would you be able to implement the proposed plan if your organization received partial funding for this project? 
Yes, we would still plan on utilizing partial funding to purchase up to 3 vehicles, however the exact number would be 
contingent upon the amount received. 
10. Describe your plan for project financial sustainability beyond the grant period.  If this is a program/project 
previously funded by the Gila River Indian Community describe efforts made towards the previously described 
sustainability plan. 
Our department is already planning ahead for the replacement of fleet vehicles that are approaching the point of being 
dangerous to operate in accordance to the requirements of a law enforcement vehicle.  This project would greatly ease 
the impending budget impact, however financial sustainability is consistently budgeted whether assistance is granted or 
not.

<!-- page 444 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2021 
 
5 
 
11. Describe your plan to document progress and results. 
Once the grant is approved by the Gila River Indian Community, the progress of this project will be sent 
directly to GRIC by the requestor. The timetable of implementation will be swift, allowing for frequent updates 
on this project. All monies received will be tracked via the Sheriff's Office Vehicle Manager and receipts for all 
purchases will be available for review by GRIC on a quarterly basis. Maintenance records will be kept by the 
Navajo County Fleet Maintenance Department and available for review.  All grant requirements will be 
consistently met. 
 
12. Indicate any application to and/or awards made by a tribe other than the Gila River Indian Community for state 
shared revenues for this and any other project for the past five (5) years.  If this information is included in a 
separate attachment, please indicate that here. 
None

IN RE THE MATTER OF:    )  ADMINISTRATIVE ORDER 
       )         NUMBER 2021-010 
APPOINTMENT OF           )   
JUVENILE HEARING OFFICER                    )   
                                                                      ) 
 
Pursuant to A.R.S. §8 -323A & B, and subject to the approval of the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors,  
 
IT IS ORDERED that Justice of the Peace Pro Tempore  David Widmaier is hereby appointed as a 
juvenile hearing officer.  The above -named shall not be pai d any compensation beyond that already 
provided pursuant to law. 
 
As a juvenile hearing officer, he may hear and dispose of all cases in which a child under the age 
of eighteen years at the time of the alleged offense is charged with violating any of the f ollowing laws 
related to: 
1. Any provision of the motor vehicle code not declared to be a felony. 
2. The purchase, possession or consumption of spirituous liquor by a juvenile. 
3. Boating or game and fish. 
4. Curfew. 
5. Truancy. 
6. The damage or disfigurement of property by  graffiti or the purchase or possession of 
materials used for graffiti. 
7. The purchase or possession of tobacco. 
8. Any city, town or political subdivision ordinance. 
9. Interference with judicial proceeding involving disobeying or resisting the lawful order, 
process or other mandate of a juvenile hearing officer or failure to appear related to any 
offense in A.R.S. §8-323. 
 
The term of these appointments shall be from March 1, 2021, through December 31, 2021.   
 
EXECUTED this 1st day of March 2021. 
 
 
              
       Dale P. Nielson, Presiding Judge

IN RE THE MATTER OF:    )  ADMINISTRATIVE ORDER 
       )         NUMBER 2021-009 
APPOINTMENT OF           )   
JUSTICE OF THE PEACE PRO TEMPORE  )   
                                         )    
       ) 
                                                                      ) 
 
The Justices of The Peace in Navajo County have requested the appointment of a Justice of Peace 
Pro Tempore to serve for the purpose of enhancing the courts ability to process cases. 
 
Whereas the following named person meets the requirements of A.R.S. §22-122, and pursuant 
to A.R.S. §22-121, and by the request of the Navajo County Justices of the Peace,  
 
IT IS ORDERED that David Widmaier is hereby appointed as Justice of the Peace Pro Tempore for 
the Navajo County Justice Courts.  The term of this appointment shall run from March 1, 2021  through 
December 31, 2021.   
 
This appointment limits the exercise of the powers granted under A.R.S. §22-121 to those 
occasions where the Justice of the Peace Pro Tempore is on Navajo County Justice Court dockets and 
not for any other purpose or function, whether mandatory or discretionary. 
 
IT IS FURTHER ORDERED, that this appointment is conditioned upon the approval of the Navajo 
County Board of Supervisors pursuant to A .R.S. §22-121, and upon the Judicial Branch obtaining 
appropriate funding from whatever source for the compensation due to said appointee pursuant to A.R.S. 
§22-123 for services as justice of the peace pro tempore. 
 
EXECUTED this 1st day of March 2021. 
 
              
       Dale P. Nielson, Presiding Judge

Motion before the Board:
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application for Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District for an
Ambulance and Gurney Addition
Background:
The purpose of the $223,225 grant would be to add an additional ambulance and gurney to the
fleet for Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District.  TMFMD was forced to place reserve
ambulances back in service to meet the service demand. This grant would replace a full-time
ambulance and return one ambulance to reserve status. The funding would also help replace one
manual gurney with a power load gurney. TMFMD ambulances are already staffed and in service,
so the implementation plan would be to order a new Type 1 4x4 ambulance, train personnel on the
new unit, have the unit inspected by AZDHS, and place the unit in service at one of our existing
stations.

Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
Narrative 
Please structure your proposal to provide the following information in the order indicated.  Provide the narrative in 
paragraph form in the text field provided.  Please be thorough but strive for brevity. 
1. Briefly describe the organization’s history, mission and goals. 
Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District (TMFMD) was formed in 2014 with the consolidation of the Show 
Low, Lakeside, and Linden Fire Districts. In 2018 TMFMD grew again when we were asked to consolidate the 
White Mountain Lake Fire District, adding 40 square miles to our service area.  TMFMD is a career Emergency 
Service organization operating out of 5 stations, with 105 employees, and covers 216 square miles in the 
communities of the White Mountains in South Navajo County.  
  
In 2018 TMFMD was also awarded an ambulance service area by AZDHS. TMFMD initially applied for 216 
square miles of service area to cover our district's residence. After the AZDHS Director reviewed our case, she 
felt that TMFMD was better equipped to serve not only our citizens but also the rural and wilderness areas 
around us. The result of that decision was TMFMD being awarded a 440 square mile ambulance service area 
bordered by White Mountain Apache Tribe to the South, Heber/Overgaard to the West, Taylor/Snowflake to 
the North, and Vernon to the East.  
 
Mission: The Timber Mesa Fire and Medical District is a public service organization dedicated to 
proactive efforts to reduce risks in the communities of the White Mountains and protect our customers' lives 
and property. 
 
2020/2023 Strategic Goals:  Strengthening Partnerships and Agreements, Employee Relations and 
Retention, Providing and Maintaining Adequate Staffing and Service Levels, Expand and Improve Medical 
Services, Providing Training to Meet the Needs of the Organization and the Community, Providing and 
Maintaining Facilities Adequate for the Organization, Services and the Community, Develop and Implement 
an Apparatus and Vehicle Replacement Program to meet the needs of the Services and the organization, 
Developing the Code Enforcement Program to Meet the Needs of the Community and Provide for Improved 
Life Safety and Maintaining and Improving the Community Risk Reduction Program to Ensure a Safer 
Community.

<!-- page 462 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
2. If the applying organization is non-municipal or a non-profit 501c, please describe your relationship with Arizona 
municipalities including past and present support, whether through partnerships, collaborations, in-kind 
contributions, grants, tax-based funding or other.  Describe the supporting municipality’s role in this project.  If 
the applying organization is a municipality governmental sub-division please enter the text “Not applicable.” 
TMFMD is a special taxing district as allowed by Arizona Law under Title 48. TMFMD is a governmental 
agency but is not under the direction of a municipality or County Board of Supervisors but instead reports to 
an elected Fire Board. TMFMD does cover the City of Show Low, part of the Town of Pinetop/Lakeside, and a 
large area of unincorporated Navajo County. TMFMD enjoys a robust relationship with all the governments 
listed above and all our other regional partners. 
3. Describe the proposed project and your plan to implement. 
In 2015 TMFMD applied for an expanded CON to serve the whole Fire District with ambulance service. In 
2018 the Director of AZDHS awarded TMFMD an expanded service area, which included the area we applied 
for and approximately 224 square miles more than was applied for, including the communities of White 
Mountain Lakes, Pinedale, Clay Springs, Vernon, and a stretch of US 60 that runs through the White 
Mountain Apache Tribe. In 2020 the private ambulance company that serviced this area shut down. The 
result of that shutdown was a loss of two ambulances in the system and an increased call volume for 
TMFMD, including responsibility for all patient transfers from Summit Hospital to other hospitals around the 
state. Due to the additional size of the CON, TMFMD has staffed an additional four full-time ambulances out 
of our current fire stations to meet the demand for service.  
TMFMD was forced to place reserve ambulances back in service to meet the service demand. This grant 
would replace a full-time ambulance and return one ambulance to reserve status. The funding would also 
help replace one manual gurney with a power load gurney. TMFMD ambulances are already staffed and in 
service, so the implementation plan would be to order a new Type 1 4x4 ambulance, train personnel on the 
new unit, have the unit inspected by AZDHS, and place the unit in service at one of our existing stations.

<!-- page 463 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
4. Describe how the proposed project satisfies one or more of the priority funding areas identified by the Gila River 
Indian Community. 
The proposed project satisfies two priority funding areas as listed in the Guidelines for Distribution of 
Tribal Funds, Healthcare and Public Safety. In Healthcare, TMFMD has greatly improved the Emergency 
Services healthcare delivery to the citizens within our district and the citizens who live in the rural and 
wilderness areas outside our district but in our ambulance service area. The previous ambulance service 
provider offered 30 min response times to these areas. TMFMD agreed to meet response times to these 
areas in 15 min or less and have successfully met that obligation. Also, TMFMD was awarded the ability to 
handle patient transport from our local hospital to higher care facilities in Flagstaff, Phoenix, and Tucson 
areas. The previous provider often left these transfers waiting for Six to 12 hours before receiving service. 
TMFMD proposed that we meet these transfer requests in one hour or less, and as of the date of this 
application, our current response times for these requests are 16 min. 
In Public Safety, there is no doubt that this new ambulance and gurney would help enhance service 
delivery to our customers, as pointed out above. Being able to replace one of our older units with a new unit 
and placing that older unit in reserve status would help meet the community’s needs and bolster the 
district’s capability to meet that need with additional reserve units. The addition of a power gurney will help 
reduce personnel injuries and help the district meet one of its employee retention goals. Lifting injuries are 
one of the most significant risks to our employees. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5. Identify the needs/problems to be addressed, target population and number of people to be served by the 
project.

<!-- page 464 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
Due to the rapid expansion of the TMFMD ambulance service area, being awarded far more area than 
was anticipated, the private ambulance provider leaving, and the COVID 19 pandemic, TMFMD was forced to 
place reserve units into primary operation to meet the need of our community. Our need is to replace an 
ambulance so that our primary units are reliable and so that one of our older units can be placed back into 
reserve status. In addition, replacing one of our older manual gurneys with a power gurney will help ensure 
that members are better equipped to provide the service and protected from unnecessary injuries.  
The project's target population would be the citizens within our 440 square mile ambulance service 
area. The area's population is approximately 34,500 full-time residence and an additional 20,500 seasonal 
residence. The additional rescue will also enhance Mutual and Automatic aid agreements to the following 
areas, Heber, Overgaard, White Mountain Apache Tribe, Vernon, Snowflake, and Taylor. 
 
6. Define the project as a new or continuing program.  Has GRIC previously funded this project? 
This project is a new. GRIC has not funded any part of this project or any other project in our district to 
date.  
7. Provide a brief timeline including start and finish dates.  Indicate if the timeline is flexible. 
If the district were notified of an intent to award this grant, we would start developing  a Request for 
Proposal. (RFP) Assuming that notification was made on July 31, 2021, it would take about one month to 
develop the RFP. One month to send out the RFP to vendors. One month for the vendors to return their bids. 
One month for the board to approve the vendor and purchase. Six months for the vendor to build the 
ambulance and deliver it. One additional month would be required for equipping the unit, DHS inspection, 
and crew training, meaning this unit would be in service around June 2022.   
 
8. Identify other organizations, partners or funders participating in the project and their roles. 
The only other organization providing funding for this project would be TMFMD. TMFMD would provide 
a Cardiac Monitor, Stair Chair, Mobile Data Terminal, Patient Care Report Tablet, Radios, Disposable Supplies, 
and other equipment as required.  
9. Would you be able to implement the proposed plan if your organization received partial funding for this project?

<!-- page 465 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
The whole plan would not be implemented with partial funding. However, part of the project could be 
implemented with partial funding. For example, TMFMD could operate a new ambulance without a power 
gurney, which would be a partial plan implementation. Also, TMFMD could equip an older ambulance with a 
power gurney, which would also implement part of the plan. Ideally, full plan funding would be the most 
beneficial to the community, our partners, and our personnel, but partial funding would meet some 
objectives.  
10. Describe your plan for project financial sustainability beyond the grant period.  If this is a program/project 
previously funded by the Gila River Indian Community describe efforts made towards the previously described 
sustainability plan. 
TMFMD currently is operating under a 3-year Strategic Plan, and one of the goals identified in that plan 
is to “Develop and Implement an Apparatus Replacement Program to meet the needs of the Services and the 
organization.” (TMFMD Strategic Plan, 2020-2023) That plan was updated when the new ambulance service 
area was awarded. However, with such rapid expansion, the district would have to purchase several 
ambulances in one year or one ambulance every year for several consecutive years, which the district does 
not have the capacity to accomplish. Once this unit is replaced, it will be placed into our apparatus 
replacement plan, meaning it will be budgeted for replacement in ten years and will serve the district for ten 
additional years as a reserve unit. 
11. Describe your plan to document progress and results. 
Every step of this project will have ample documentation. Starting with the grant application and the 
project plan described in it. If we were to be awarded, the unit would then be moved into our apparatus 
working group, where the unit would be designed. That design would then be documented in an RFP and 
submitted to vendors for their bids. Once the bids were received, the purchase would be placed on a board 
agenda and approved for purchase. A contract would then be signed by the district and the vendor for the 
purchase. Once the unit is received, the unit will be equipped, stocked, and inspected by AZDHS. When the 
unit is inspected, personnel will be trained on the unit and documented in our training program, Target 
Solutions. Then the unit will be placed in service. From that point on, all activities performed on the unit will 
be tracked in our data management software, including calls, maintenance, equipment checks, public safety 
programs, and education programs conducted in the unit.

<!-- page 466 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2020 
 
Once the unit is placed in service, TMFMD will perform program performance evaluations regularly. 
These evaluations are performed to show call volumes, response times, and customer satisfaction is meeting 
the standard as required by AZDHS and NFPA. 
12. Indicate any application to and/or awards made by a tribe other than the Gila River Indian Community for state 
shared revenues for this and any other project for the past five (5) years.  If this information is included in a 
separate attachment, please indicate that here. 
TMFMD has not been awarded any tribal grants from state shared revenues for the past 5 years.

 Project Budget Detail 
Please provide a narrative description for each of the project budget items listed on the previous page.  Include the 
dollar figure and how it was derived. 
1. Type 1 4X4 Ambulance to replace one of our front-line Type 1 4X4 ambulances so that it can be moved into reserve 
status. The proposed budget item is $212,957 and derived from a recent ambulance purchase, inflation, spec changes, 
and sales tax. That purchase was for a demo Type 1 4X4 ambulance, and the budgeted amount was $175,000. This unit 
will be built for us, meaning that there will be additional cost for items that we will spec into the truck. The assumed 
amount of those specs are $4,500. This number also assumes that inflation will drive up the cost of this unit, up to 10% 
for an amount of $17,500. Finally, the in-kind contributions assume an 8.1% sales tax. The district will cover any tax 
required at a cost of up to $15,957.   
2. A Power Load gurney will be purchased to replace one of our existing manual load gurneys. This purchase's proposed 
budget is $30,268 and was derived from a recent power gurney purchase, inflation, and sales tax. That purchase was for 
a power gurney, which cost $23,841. Assuming inflation will be up to 10%, an additional $2,384 was added to that 
purchase to come up with the total amount requested of $26,225. Finally, the in-kind contributions assume an 8.1% 
sales tax. The district will cover any tax required at a cost of up to $2,124.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. m.        
Meeting Date: 03/09/2021  
Title: DEM PC March 9
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Martha Pearce and Jonathan Michael Nez as Democratic Precinct Committeemen
for their respective precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2020. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy;
duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an election to fill
precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of precinct committeeman
positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election for those positions not sooner
than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the person who filed the nominating
petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a nominating petition to fill a position, the
position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A precinct
committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this subsection after filing a nominating petition shall
be deemed an elected precinct committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is
two years and begins on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman
was a candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. n.        
Meeting Date: 03/09/2021  
Title: Appointment of Taber Heisler and Re-appointment of Randy Murph to the Planning
and Zoning Commission
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Taber Heisler for a two-year term and re-appointment of Randy Murph for a
four-year term to the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission
Background:
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission advises and makes recommendations to the
Board of Supervisors on matters of planning, zoning, and subdivision platting.  Typical issues
considered by the Commission include re-zoning requests, Special Use Permits, and Zoning
Ordinance amendments.  Currently, there is a vacancy on the Commission in District V, and
Commissioner Murph's term expired on January 31, 2021 and is being considered for
re-appointment.
For the District V vacancy, Taber Heisler has expressed strong interest in serving on the
Commission.  In his letter of interest (attached to this item), Mr. Heisler states that serving on the
Commission "provides an excellent opportunity to serve" his community.  His appointment has
been reviewed and approved by Vice Chairwoman Whitesinger.
For the District III term expiration, Commissioner Murph has expressed interest in being
re-appointed.  While serving on the Commission, Commissioner Murph has been a valuable,
dependable and knowledgable representative for District III.  His request has been reviewed and
approved by Supervisor Whiting.
Staff supports the appointment of Taber Heisler for a two-year term, and re-appointment of Randy
Murph for a four-year term to the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission.
